| 508 | When getting grabbed by a giant my camera shakes a lot, like it teleports back and forth | Held, a player was put where the giant held them every frame by a teleport -- and a teleport ends the easing of the view between one step of the simulation and the next, so the eye jumped from step to step, back and forth against the frames drawn in between. Held already, now only where they are held moves, and the step carries them there, eased as walking is. |
| 509 | When shooting you can see far away way too well | Each shot lit up the whole night: the land to its horizon, the buildings, the sky. Not the flash's own light, which reaches eleven metres -- its glare: its brightness was held from climbing only within a third of a metre of it, and the weapon is a hand span from it, so the weapon and the ground under it were lit three hundred times over, and that blaze's bloom washed over the whole picture. It is held within most of a metre now, and the flash lights the room about a quarter as brightly: still the brightest thing about, lighting the walls round you, not the land a hundred metres off. |
| 510 | You shouldn't be able to equip items while sitting | Sitting down puts away what was in the hands, and nothing can be taken out -- by its number or the wheel -- until you stand. |
| 511 | For the arrival cutscene the planet's name should only show up when the camera flips round to view it | Its name comes up as the camera has come round behind the ship and the world is filling the picture (8.6 s), not before. |
| 512 | During the landing transition the place where you are going to land looks like it glows blue | Coming down, the globe is painted round the landing place in the colour the land there is from up high, so land and globe meet without a seam; that colour is worked out from the land's far colour over the light on the globe, and could come out several times too bright -- a pale blue glow round the place. It is kept within reach of the world's own colour there now. |
| 513 | When flying the ship back the snow texture scrolls when the ship is moving | The patches of snow lying on things were laid out by where the thing was in the world: on the ship taking off, the snow slid over its hull as it went. They are laid out on the thing itself now (the ground and everything built into a site are drawn where they are, so nothing changes there). |
| 514 | The little edges that build up on buildings: if the building is off the floor they just stop | The drifts of snow or sand built against a building's walls were built against one standing on stilts too -- rising to walls that start up in the air, and stopping short at its piles, their edge hanging under its floor. None is built against one on stilts now. |
| 515 | It was not spawning creatures on missions, and it would not let me spawn one (the second contract) | Near home a place was as likely as not to have nothing living there, the place with a contract as much as any other -- and now that only contracts have work (FIX 496), a contract's site empty was often. Where there is work -- a contract, or a finding that led there -- something lives there now nearly always (from 85% near home); anywhere else is as empty as before. And spawn_creature, asked for a spot that is no good (up on a crate, over a drop), looks further round it -- twelve metres, then anywhere walkable within twenty -- instead of giving up, and says which it was when it cannot: nothing here to walk on (aboard the ship, or a place still being made), already the most there can be, or no ground near there. |
| 516 | Creatures can hurt or grab you through walls if big enough and you are close to a wall | A blow was checked from the creature's head only: a big one pressed against a wall with its head pushed through it bit and took hold of somebody on the far side. Its body has to be able to reach them now as well as its head. |
| 517 | Get rid of the sounds step_light 4-6 | Gone, with their recording's line in Docs/CREDITS.md (the other light steps are untouched). |
| 518 | My gun and hands are shaking while just holding still | The one thing found shaking the view and what is held while standing still is a giant's tread: each of its footfalls shook the picture of anybody within four times its size -- a big one walking a couple of hundred metres off kept the weapon and the hands trembling. It is felt within one and a half times its size now, close enough to see it is what is shaking you. (A held weapon also sways more while you are hurt, on purpose.) |
| 519 | When dying, the giant's head clipped into the ground and its legs kept sliding, so the screen kept shaking; it didn't ragdoll well | Dead, a strider came down and bounced, and bounced: each bounce a footfall shaking the picture, its feet sliding out for ever under it, its hull lowered so far its head went into the ground. It comes down once now -- the ground taking its weight heard and felt the one time -- and lies still, its hull resting high enough that the head lies on the ground, not in it, the head no longer pitched down into it; and its feet slide out as fast as it is big as it falls, so its legs splay out under it rather than leaving its knees standing high over it. |
| 520 | Still able to get both airlock doors open at the same time; a light was floating outside the airlock; is that power supply meant to be there? | The airlocks of every building but the first on a site were never paired: when a site gathers its buildings' doors into one list, each airlock door still named its other by its place in its own building's list, so in the second and third buildings each was paired with some door of the first -- and both of its own could be opened together. They are moved on with the list now (a test checks every airlock on sites of several buildings: 184 wrong pairings before). The floating light: each airlock door has a warning light on the wall either side, put on the wall found there -- and where no wall was found (the corner of an airlock built out from the wall, past where it ends) it was put in the air anyway; there is none there now. The cabinet is meant to be there: it is a mission's feeder cabinet (a building's feed cut, switched over to its standby at it) -- but it was set three and a half metres out from a door, which with an airlock built out round the door is across the corner of the airlock's far door; beside an airlock it stands against the building's wall now, clear of it. |
| 521 | I want a new kind of door for airlocks and improve them visually | A way out's airlock has pressure doors now, both of them: a thick steel slab that slides sideways into the wall rather than swinging, all but a lip of it -- framed by a raised rim held by bolts, two ribs across it, hazard chevrons across its foot and head, a tall narrow port in a heavy frame at the eye, a black seal down its leading edge and a long pull bar by it; it is worked at the doorway it leaves when open. (A clean room's airlock, too narrow to slide one into its wall, keeps its hinged door; a vehicle bay's keeps its shutters.) Inside every airlock: a louvred vent low in each side wall with ducts along the top of the wall dropping to it, a drain grate in the floor, and a second control panel by the far door. |
| 522 | The creatures are seeing me through doors and their heads are clipping through them (waiting by the door, its head through the port) | What a creature sees from is its eyes, kept on its own side of anything solid between its chest and its head -- but "solid" was the level alone: doors are bodies that move, so a creature standing at a shut door with its head pushed out past the panel had its eyes on the far side of the door, and saw whoever was there. Its head was kept out of walls the same way, so nothing kept it out of a door: through the port it went. Doors are as solid as walls now to its eyes and to its head -- drawn back to this side of the panel, a snout's length clear of it -- and so to what it sees. |
| 523 | Creature behaviour: it attacked, I shot, it walked away while being shot, then slept not far off in the open; ran away and came back to attack from the same angle; came back to the airlock where I last was and looped that forever | Shot at, a creature does go for whoever shot it even unseen (it knows where the shot came from). What followed was the trouble. Tired, it lay down wherever it was if there was no nook within 25 m -- out in the open, wounded or not; hurt in the last four minutes now, it never lies down in the open nor anywhere near where it was hurt: it goes well away and tucks itself in somewhere sheltered first. Its approach under somebody's eyes was always straight down their sights; now (FIX 524) it comes on a slant, side to side, so it never comes the same way twice. And a door it could not get through (an airlock, interlocked) it gave up for thirty seconds -- then came back to it, and back, for ever. Given up again, the same door is left two minutes, then eight; and with whoever it wants on the far side of such a door, it no longer goes at them through it: it waits beside the door, flat against the wall off to the side of it (not in front of the port), for them to come out. |
| 524 | Make all creatures able to strafe, walk sideways, wrap around you; improve stalking so they play around what the player can see and do, and use walls and ceilings smartly (switch to the ceiling to stay out of gunfire) | Hunting somebody who is looking at it, a creature no longer runs straight down their sights: it comes in on a slant, from one side and then the other, every second or two, circling in on them as it closes -- and within fourteen metres it keeps its eyes on them as it goes, moving sidelong, crabwise round them. Shot at as it comes on indoors, one that can climb goes up the wall and comes on across the ceiling -- out of the line of fire at a body's height -- and drops onto them when it gets there. (Stalking already went for where they are not looking and round behind them; packs already split, one in front holding their eyes.) |
| 525 | Improve the sleep mechanic, give it a better animation | A creature going to sleep crouched where it stood. Now it settles first, as an animal does -- turning round once on the spot, slowly, getting lower -- and then lies down: on its belly, over a little onto one side, its feet drawn in under it, its body curled round to that side and its head laid down and round along its flank, breathing slow and deep (its back rising and falling), the odd small dreaming twitch. Woken, it is up again in a moment. Every machine sees the same (it is sent with what it is doing). |
| 526 | Many giants just look at you, and if you shoot them they don't react or attack you | A strider looked at from more than 40 m off stands dead still for as long as it is looked at -- and what broke that was its being angry enough, which one shot never made it: shot by somebody looking at it (as you are, shooting), it went straight back to standing still. Shot now, it never stands still to be looked at again for a minute and a half, it is at once angry enough to hunt in earnest, and it comes for whoever shot it straight away -- out of standing still, out of looking about where they went in. Tested: shot at 135 m, it came, round among the buildings and in, and took the shooter. |
| 527 | Don't count everything the giants do as playing: they are hunting | What a giant does over somebody, sizing them up and closing in before it strikes, is hunting now -- the mode's name, everything it says it is doing ("over it, sizing it up", "missed; after it again", "done with it; threw it away"), and every word about it in the code. |
| 528 | Giants should only grab you with their tentacles, and just step on you if they don't have tentacles | Only a strider with strands under it takes anybody now, with a strand. One with none raises a foot -- seen coming -- and stamps it down on them: a heavy blow, never a hold, the ground shaking with it. (Its test now expects a stamp from one with no strands.) |
| 529 | The giants' tentacles clip into the ground more than anything | Hanging their whole length (as long as the strider is tall), their lower parts went into the ground. They lie on the ground where they reach it now, never into it. |
| 530 | The giants' leg placement isn't always great; all creatures should be able to strafe and wrap around you, giants too | Its feet were put down where its stride said, pushed further out if that was on a roof. Each now takes the best of fifteen places about there -- turned a little round the hip, in or out a little -- never on or in anything built, never with the leg going down through a building's wall, on the flattest ground near where it would go; and each step is lifted clear over whatever is built under the way the foot swings. What its belly clears it now steps over (it stood a hundred metres off among buildings, pushed off each in turn, and came no nearer); and getting no nearer for six seconds, it finds a way round. Closing in, it comes round you sidelong, its eyes on you, one way and then now and then the other; over you, before it strikes, it shifts round you, cutting off the way you would run. |
| 531 | The giants' heads all look like bugs, which I don't like | The head was a long tapering snout with mandibles or a beak at its end and a row of up to eight bead eyes down its sides: an insect's. It is a skull now -- a domed braincase, a long muzzle, heavy brows over two deep sockets with the eyes sunk in them looking forward, cheekbones, a mouth cut the length of the muzzle with teeth down it and a long one near the front, nostril slits at its end; on some a ridge down the skull and horns swept back from behind the brows, on a few a smaller pair of eyes over the first. Each its own in the length of its muzzle, the height of its dome and the weight of its brows. (giant_mind now also says where its head is.) |
| 532 | Improve the leviathans visually, more realistic; not all serpents but the same vibe as serpents; its face and edges are very low resolution, it doesn't feel like a threat or very smart | The whole leviathan was made on one grid, set by its body -- for one forty to a hundred and forty metres long, a few dozen cells across the whole head, so its face came out soft and lumpy. The head and its jaw are made on a grid of their own now, about five times finer, joined to the body behind it under its skin. And the head itself is new: long, low and wedge-shaped, a viper's -- widest at the hinge of its jaws, where cheeks swell out, narrowing to a pointed snout with nostril slits; a flat skull with a low plated ridge down it; brows slanting down over each eye, a frown, the eyes sunk in sockets under them (a smaller pair behind on some); a long lower jaw, rows of teeth down both and a fang near the front. Two new kinds with a serpent's look beside the five serpents: a paddled sea reptile, thicker, with four flippers and a fluke at its tail, and a sea dragon, with long whiskers trailing from its snout, a mane of spines down its neck and fins at its shoulders. A frill's spines are stout enough now to come out whole. |
| 533 | Give the leviathan more animations: it's not opening its mouth | Its lower jaw is a part of its own now, on a hinge at the back of its mouth: nearly shut as it goes about, breathing water through it; parted and working as it hunts; a little open as it gathers itself to strike, wide as it comes, snapping shut on the bite; slack, dead. The same on every machine. (Its test now checks the jaws open wide whenever it bites.) |
| 534 | The leviathan feels dumb: it just swims around me and can't seem to get to me, going around and through me | Three things. Before it struck it tried to get round behind whoever it was after -- and they turn to face it, so behind them moved and it went round and round, for up to 25 seconds; now, close with them not looking its way, it comes from where it is, and after nine seconds of being turned to it comes anyway. Its strike was aimed at a point six metres past them, so a rush that missed carried on through them and away; it goes for where they will be now, and gone past them -- dodged -- it turns and comes again in a second or two instead of circling off. And its body was not solid to anybody: it swam through people. Whoever is in the water with it is pushed out of its body, the nearest way out, and carried along by it as it passes. |
| 535 | Make the leviathan more aware of its body: it swims into objects and clips through them | It kept away from what was built, but nothing else: rocks, the sea's floor rising, the posts of things. It now feels ahead of its head along where it is going, a body length and a half on, for anything solid, and takes the clearest way round -- up, down or to either side. (Its body follows the way its head went.) giant_view puts the camera by a giant's head to look at it. |
| 536 | Still having an issue at surface level with the water: it's not rendering the clouds or other stuff through it | With the eye under the surface -- even a hand's breadth under, as a swimmer's is half the time -- the surface overhead was drawn as one flat sheet of bright murk, nothing of the world over it showing. Looked up at steeply enough, it is now a window on the world over it, as a real surface is from under it (Snell's window): the whole sky over it squeezed into a circle overhead out to about 49 degrees from straight up -- the sky, its clouds drifting, the sun -- broken up and wavering with the waves, dimmer the deeper the eye; past its edge the surface is a mirror of the water under it, darker. (Not under a roof.) |
| 537 | In underwater facilities the water lights are on the floor if it's close to the top, which should not happen | The net of light the waves gather on what lies under them (caustics) was put on anything under the sea's surface facing up -- a sealed room's floor on the sea's floor too, where it was near the surface. Only where the open sky reaches it now: never inside. |
| 538 | If you have any more cool or scary/creepy mechanics for the creatures, add them (1: lamps) | Lamps stutter near something creeping. A creature stalking, lying in wait or luring troubles every lamp within six and a half metres of it: the lamp drops nearly out in short, irregular bursts, more often the nearer it is -- the light in the corridor ahead going, coming back, going, with nothing to be seen. A warning to the watchful, and a fright to everybody else. The same on every machine. |
| 539 | (2: giants) | A strider waiting about where somebody went in under a roof no longer only waits. Every nine seconds or so it feels at the way in with one of its strands, tapping at it -- or, with none, raises a foot and stamps it down beside the way in, the ground and the walls shaking with it. Nobody indoors is ever reached by it. It gives up after 45 seconds in all, however much stamping there was in between (each stamp had started its waiting over). Its changes of mind are logged now, as a creature's are. |
| 540 | (3: leviathans) | The first time a leviathan comes to somebody in the water, it does not come for them: it goes by under them -- slowly, a few of its widths down, its lights passing beneath them -- seen, or only felt, before it ever comes for them. Then it hunts. (Not when it has been shot: then it comes.) |
| 541 | If a giant spawns, there need to be giant signs as well | Where a giant lives, the organism's signs are as big as it is now: two or three formations beside the rest, about three times the size, their branches as thick as trunks, along its way in from where it keeps to the site -- out on the land for a strider, on the sea's floor for a leviathan. |
| 542 | The signs glow, this should not be the case | On the organism's rhythm the formations glowed with the creature's light. They give no light of their own now -- they swell harder with the rhythm, and the lamps still dip with it where it is strongest. (The glow's colour, worked out for every site and used by nothing else, is gone with it.) |
| 543 | Make the leviathan heads look more like these (pictures: heavy toothed sea-monster heads) | Not a copy of any of them -- what they have in common, on its own head: heavier, a taller skull and a deep, bony lower jaw instead of a flat wedge; a pair of long fangs at the front of each jaw, thick at the root, the upper hanging past the lower lip and the lower standing up in front of the upper, and more and bigger teeth down both jaws behind them; two eyes each side, the second a little behind and below the first, sunk under brows knobbed with bone; bony spikes down the side of the lower jaw, pointing down and back; a horn swept back from behind each brow and a spine under it. Its mouth hangs open more -- a little going about, wider as it hunts -- showing its teeth and the dark red inside, no longer near black. (giant_view quarter looks at a giant from in front and to one side of where its head points.) |
| 544 | The giants walking on buildings: their body should go up onto the top of the building | A strider only ever put its feet on the ground and its body at its height over them -- crossing a building, its legs went round it and its body could be let down into the roof (bending down to look, or the ground falling away). It now walks over buildings as a thing that size would: a foot can come down on a roof, well in from its edges, and its body rises with its feet as it climbs up onto the building and over it; whatever its feet, its belly is always kept clear of the roof under it; and dead over a building, it comes down on the roof. (Not on a half-fallen building, whose roof is broken.) giant_mind says how many of its feet are up on roofs. Tested: crossing a building to where somebody had gone in, two to four of its five feet were up on the roof. |
| 545 | Bring back the old arrival cutscene where the camera doesn't move around the ship; the new one I don't like as much | Back as it was before FIX 493: the still shot from behind and above the ship with the world before it, fourteen seconds, the world's name coming up near its end (10.2 s). |
| 546 | I don't know why, but day_hour isn't working: it stays dark | It moved the clock to the hour asked for at the place, and said so -- but far enough towards a pole on a tilted world there are times of the year the sun never rises, so at noon it was still night. Asked for an hour of daylight where it is polar night just then (or of night in a midnight sun), it now goes on through the year a day at a time to the first day the sun is up (or down) at that hour, and says that it did; and it says the sun's height in the log. On a test site, which is no place on a world, it did nothing to the site's own sky: it now sets that sky's hour -- the sun from the east at six to the west at eighteen, the light of the sky, the ground and the fog going with it. |
| 547 | Rework and improve the flashlight: make it turn to where you are looking (you pick which is best); it still lights stuff up and renders stuff in the distance weirdly | Picked: aimed where you look, every frame -- as a lamp worn on the head is -- not swinging after the view as one held at arm's length, which left its beam lighting where you had been looking a moment before (its catching-up setting, r.torch_follow, is gone). And in fog, dust or haze its beam no longer fills the middle of the view with a bright disc: the light the air throws back is less than half what it was, and none of it is the glare of the few centimetres in front of the lens, which was most of the disc -- the beam shows as a shaft out into the murk, the same in the sky, on the ground and on the water. |
| 548 | The fog should be tied to the weather as well, and in more places where it fits | Fog comes and goes now with the weather and the place, the same for everybody: on a world of fog its banks roll in and thin out; rain and snow close the distance in as they come on, rain the more where it is damp; early mornings are foggy where it is damp -- jungle and sea worlds most, temperate country, toxic and storm worlds, by a site's lakes or on its coast -- and freezing on cold worlds; and a light haze lies over damp ground at night. At its thickest the distance is lost at sixty-odd metres (thinned on a log scale, as the eye judges distance: half thick, a few hundred metres), the fog the grey of the light it is in, the sun dimmed through it and the ground mist in the hollows with it. None without air or on the sea's floor. |
| 549 | Still when getting grabbed the camera flips back and forth quickly (it was the giants) | Held by a giant, the view itself was steady -- but its strands hung right across your face, the one holding you wrapped at the height of your eyes, and swung back and forth through the picture the whole time. The one that holds you now coils round your middle, and every other strand is kept a couple of metres off your eyes while you are held. (Held by a creature, too, the view is now turned steadily to its face: eased, never at a point nearer than two metres, not swung round when its eye is nearly overhead, and its jolting a slow sway rather than a judder eleven times a second.) |
| 550 | The giants: I don't like how they pick you up and just put you down | Lifted to its eyes and turned over, you are no longer lowered and let go. It eats you -- hungry, angry, or having smashed you once already -- or it swings you down fast and smashes you into the ground: badly hurt (45), knocked away along it, the ground shaking with it; and it comes for you again at once. Struggling free or shooting it off still works throughout. (Its test now expects the smash.) |
| 551 | You can't visually see the tentacle doing anything besides wiggling | Only its tip was pulled about; the rest of it hung, so all that showed was a wiggle. The strand at work is drawn to what it is doing now: hunting, reaching out towards you most of the way and feeling about there, raised and swaying; striking, drawn back and up as it winds up, then whipped down in an arch onto you; holding, run down to you and coiled round your middle, turning slowly. |
| 552 | For the leviathans, make it so they swim away and then flank you from far away so you can't see what they are doing | A leviathan no longer circles close by where it can be watched. Coming to somebody in the water it turns away from forty-five metres out, and hunting them: swims off, dark, out of sight into the murk (fifty metres or more, more the bigger it is); out there, unseen, goes round to behind where they are looking; and then comes in from behind and a little below, fast and dark, its rush and its bite only once it is close. Missed, it goes off again and comes round once more. (Its test now gives it two minutes, to go off and round before it comes.) |
| 553 | The leviathan's teeth and stuff turn into white splotches in the mouth: make it a lot higher quality and finer | Its teeth and fangs were carved out of the same grid as its head, one or two of its cells across, and melted into blobs. Each is a cone of its own now -- smooth, sharp to its point at any size, sunk a little into the gum, ivory darkening to the root -- the upper jaw's on its head, the lower jaw's on the jaw, opening with it. |
| 554 | Improve the mesh maker for the creatures: they look a little low quality and not very good up close | Their skins are sampled a fifth finer (the grid the body is made on: about two-thirds of a centimetre in a hundred of its length) and keep more of it once simplified: ten to twenty-two thousand triangles where it was six and a half to sixteen. A body still builds in well under a tenth of a second. |
| 555 | Give me a list of everything the creatures can do, including the giants and leviathans, so I can test them; and if you can, add a way that makes it easier to test | Docs/CREATURE_TESTING.md: every tool for it, and everything the creatures, giants and leviathans do -- what they sense, each behaviour and how to see it, how they move and learn, their quirks; a strider's freeze, approach over buildings, hunting, strike or stamp, hold, waiting at a building, death; a leviathan's first pass, its hunt out of sight and from behind, its strike, its body and jaws -- and things worth trying. And two commands: `creature_do <behaviour> [n] [seconds]` makes the creature nearest you (or number n) do any of its behaviours now, at you -- put straight into it where it needs nothing first, otherwise wanting it above all else so that it does it its own way (somewhere to wait, a body, a voice) -- or lie down to sleep; and `giant_do <roam|notice|approach|hunt|strike|linger>` puts the giant into a mode, after you. Both tab-complete. |
| 556 | Get rid of the leviathan going under you slowly: it doesn't have the effect we want and is kind of just bad | Gone (FIX 540's pass under): a leviathan comes to somebody in the water and goes straight into its hunt -- off into the murk and round. |
| 557 | The leviathan, wrapping around and attacking me, went through a building and didn't make any noise when attacking | Its head is pushed off anything built that it would swim into (a ring round it at its own width, felt for), up and over where it can, and its body follows its head's way; and with something solid between it and whoever it rushes at, it gives the rush up and goes off and round again rather than coming on through. Its rush is heard now -- a roar coming at you through the water -- and its bite as it lands. |
| 558 | The leviathan's jaws don't smoothly connect to their body | Its lower jaw was cut off the head along a plane: open, a hard edge and a gap showed where they met. The jaw now reaches a little up into the head and back past its hinge, narrowing there: shut, that is inside the head; open, it fills where the gap was, so the jaw runs into the head. |
| 559 | The giants move their limbs really fast and kind of weirdly when on top of a building | Every step took the same short time, however far it had to go -- up over a building, up onto its roof -- so a leg whipped fifteen metres up and down in under a second. A step now takes as long as it has far to go. |
| 560 | The tentacles clip into buildings and aren't very smooth a lot of the time; they bunch up weirdly | They lie over a roof now rather than going into a building; they keep a few of their widths apart from each other instead of collapsing into one bundle; and each is smoothed along itself every frame, no kinks. |
| 561 | Get rid of the stuff for the creatures that makes them look like bugs | Gone: mandibles (pincers either side of the mouth), feelers off the brow, and the hooked stinger at the tail's end (FIX 494's). Their draws are still taken from each creature's seed, so nothing else about any creature changed with them. |
| 562 | Improve the walk animation: they don't feel like they're stepping onto the leg, and sometimes the legs sync for a bit so it hops | A foot left a little behind stepped whatever the others were doing -- at a run that was often, every leg in the air at once: a hop (24 to 41 per cent of the time, measured). Now only one badly behind steps out of turn, and it is under 5 per cent (a test checks five kinds of body walking and running). And its weight goes onto its legs: each foot put down sinks the body a little as it takes the weight, springing back, deeper the faster it goes, in place of an even rise and fall that had nothing to do with its feet; and it leans over onto the side of the feet that are down. |
| 563 | I'm still getting "no walkable ground" sometimes when I load in a test site and then spawn_creature | Just after a site is made, the ground the creatures walk on is still being mapped in the background (the first map, of a few hundred pieces, is replaced a couple of seconds later by the whole site's) -- and a creature asked for meanwhile, somewhere the first map did not reach, was refused. Asked for while the ground is still being mapped, it now comes as soon as the mapping is done ("The ground here is still being mapped: it will come as soon as it is"). |
| 564 | Got up on a tank and the creature's AI got stuck (pressed into the side of the tank under me, its head in the wall) | Somebody higher than it reaches counted as out of reach only when the map of the ground had no way up -- and a tank's ladder is a way up on the map, so it kept going for them, into the tank's side. Higher than it reaches is out of reach now, whatever the map says: one that climbs goes up the side after them; anything else paces round the foot of it -- at its own level, never a spot up there with them -- calling the others, and in the end goes to wait somewhere they will have to come down to. |
| 565 | I did snow at 1 and it didn't look like it was snowing much, and the sky was very visible | At its heaviest a snowfall only brought the distance in to three hundred-odd metres, under a dark cloud deck that was drawn over the picture after the fog, so it stood clear over the snow. Now: twice the flakes and the air thicker with them; the distance down to seventy metres at the heaviest (taken on a log scale; dust forty-five, ash ninety, rain three hundred); the fog of snow and dust pale -- lit through, white-grey -- not a dark blue murk; the sky lost in it; and the cloud deck fades out as the fog closes in below its base, so in a blizzard there is only the grey. |
| 566 | On a new campaign the weather should be clear at the start | No weather at all -- rain, snow, dust, ash or the fog that comes with the weather and the damp -- for a campaign's first twenty minutes, coming in over the next ten. |
| 567 | In the navigation map I'm moving my camera and it snaps to a planet even when I'm not close: the planet gets really close | The camera was kept in front of any body that lay anywhere along the way back from what it looked at (FIX 502) -- so turning the view until a planet passed behind it pulled the camera right in to that planet. It is kept out of a body only where it would actually be inside it or right against it, which was the case that cost the frames. |
| 568 | Still a floating light outside airlocks | On a world with airlocks, the lamp over each way out was moved out onto the far wall of the airlock built out from the wall there -- but since airlocks have been built inside the building where there is room (FIX 483), many ways out have nothing outside them, and their lamp was moved out four metres into the air anyway, its bracket reaching back to nothing. Only a way out with an airlock built out from the wall moves its lamp now. |
| 569 | The airlocks are messed up: they cut off windows | An airlock built out from the wall covered, or cut off at its side, a window beside its door. No window is put on the ground floor where an airlock's whole width would come, and a hand more. |
| 570 | The airlocks are too close to walls (a narrow slot between an airlock's wall and the room's) | An airlock built inside the building was its own size in whatever room it was in -- in one only a little bigger, a strip a metre or less was left between its walls and the room's: a slot behind it or beside it. Where the room's walls are that near, it is built out to them now (the room measured in from the door and along either side of it). |
| 571 | Make sure everything scales correctly when the window changes size; on the intro's fade-in everything feels too zoomed in | The whole interface -- menus, the HUD, the console, the overlays and fades -- is laid out for a picture 1080 pixels high and drawn bigger or smaller as the window is taller or shorter (never under 0.8 or over twice), its letters drawn sharp at the window's own resolution and the pointer brought into the same scale. A setting, ui.scale (0.5 to 2), makes it all bigger or smaller on top of that. |
| 572 | I'm changing the fog commands and they're not working: check all the commands and make sure they work | r.fog_start and r.fog_end only ever set a starting value that every site, its weather and its hour then replaced, so changing them did nothing. They win now, over everything, whenever they are set (-1, the default, leaves the world's own); the debug sliders go from -1. r.site_fog_scale pushes the fog out at any site, after its weather. And a new command, site_fog <0 to 1 / world> (or r.site_fog), sets how foggy the site is, over its weather and the hour. Checked in a test run: the world's own 60 to 4132 m; site_fog 1, 2 to 65 m; r.site_fog_scale 3, three times that; r.fog_start 5 and r.fog_end 30, exactly that. |
| 573 | With the weather I can see too much of the sky through the fog; turn the fog down for a lot of the weather -- rain shouldn't really have any fog | Rain is hardly a fog now: the far hills greyed in a downpour, a kilometre or so off, the near ground clear (it came down to 300 m, and the mist off wet ground with it to a few hundred); an overcast sky takes less of the distance too. Under rain its cloud closes the whole sky, and no stars show through any weather or an overcast at night -- they shone through once the deck had faded into a blizzard's or a downpour's grey. Snowflakes are lit as the white air round them is, never darker: they were dark blue specks against a blizzard. Snow, dust and ash keep their thick murk (a blizzard down to a few tens of metres). |
| 574 | The tentacles spasm out a bit and don't look like they connect at the top of the body | The strand doing the work was picked afresh every frame as the nearest -- two of them swapped back and forth, each snapping to the reach and back; it is chosen once as the reach, strike or hold starts and kept to its end. Pushing them apart and smoothing them gave them a speed they kept, and they twitched; that no longer happens. Each tube's rings are carried on along it rather than set from a fixed direction, which flipped them round and twisted the tube as it swung. And each grows out of the skin as it is drawn -- its root in the belly as it bends, breathes and swings, its first link straight out of it, thickening into it -- not where the body would be held still, which stood it off the skin. |
| 575 | (AI review Q1) Usually one creature, but there can be groups of them if they are smaller | A site's creatures are one now, whatever their size, except the smaller kinds: tiny ones in a group of three or four (three times in five), small ones two or three (two times in five). A usual-sized one is never two any more. |
| 576 | (Q5) Your voice should attract them only when talking loudly | Talking at an ordinary level is not heard by them any more. A raised voice or a shout is, from further the louder (six to fifteen metres) -- your own measured from your microphone, everybody else's from their voice as it arrives (every frame of it now decoded, in order, whether or not it is near enough to be heard here). |
| 577 | (Q4, my pick) How far they see | Out in open daylight they now see you from more than twice as far -- about sixty metres for most -- where they were blind at thirty in an open field. Under a roof, or at night, as before (twenty-six). And out under the sky at night you are no longer as lit as in daylight to their eyes: the open air is as light as the sky is. |
| 578 | (Q18) Keep the giant's freeze when watched, but only sometimes: right now it is mostly all the time | About half of striders freeze when looked at from far off at all, and one that does, about two times in three -- decided as it is first looked at and kept for forty seconds. The rest keep coming while you watch. |
| 579 | (Q15) Learning shared by all of them | What the creatures learn of the crew -- which ways of going at you have worked (got blows in, a grab) and which have cost them (shot, killed) -- was shared by everything at a site and forgotten as you left it. It is kept with the campaign now: every creature, of every kind, at every site, leans on what all the others found out. |
| 580 | (Q10) Make stalking like Lethal Company: I've never been stalked by one, I always see it | A stalker is not there to be looked at. Caught in your sight it no longer stops and stares back at you: it slips away out of sight, fast, and keeps further off for a while (ten to thirty metres). Looked at too long all the same (four to seven seconds, by its patience), or come right up to, it is angered: it stands stock still, staring, for a moment, roars and comes for you. With your back turned it creeps up behind you, unseen, and goes for you only from close -- the stealthy ones no longer charge across the open where you would turn and see them coming. It waits longer for its chance (twelve to forty seconds). Fixed on the way: a creature could never creep up behind anybody at all (the distance it measured was always one metre); stopped a step short of its hiding place, it never counted itself in cover and never looked out; it never looked again for somebody it had last seen facing it; and shot, it decided what to do before it was frightened by it. |
| 581 | (Q12) Grabs kind of feel pointless: make them better, and only larger creatures that could grab you can | Only a creature heavy enough to carry a grown person off grabs now (140 kg or more of it, and only some of those). And taken is no longer only being moved: it bites you now and then as it drags you off, away from the others, and there it pins you down and mauls you -- a bite every second or so, enough to kill you -- until you struggle free (jump, over and over), somebody shoots it off you, or you are dead. It no longer throws you down and lets go. |
| 582 | (Q22) I like that the giants pick you up; I don't want them to just put you down: maybe they throw you | Lifted up and looked at, you are no longer smashed into the ground: it draws you back and up beside it, swings round and throws you -- out across the ground, fifteen-odd metres -- hurt by the throw and the landing (about seventy in all: badly hurt, alive). Then it comes for you again; thrown once already, the next time it eats you. |
| 583 | (Q8) Hiding in vents, and getting about by them as well | A creature whose body fits the vents (the crawlspaces) now goes about by them now and then -- through them to where it is going, near itself or near where people go -- and lies up in them a while, listening, before it moves on. Stalking, it hides in them too, near whoever it is after, looking out (a vent counts as better hiding than anywhere else). And it is heard in them: a hollow knock and a scrape of the duct's metal every few seconds as it moves (a new sound, World/vent_thump) -- less often the more careful it is being, never while it creeps up on somebody. Not into the vents to get out of a storm. A test has one go into the vents and lie up there with nobody about. |
| 584 | (Q21, my pick) Should a giant reach into buildings? | Yes, a little: waiting where you went in, with you still just inside -- a few metres in, in line with the doorway, nothing between -- a strider with strands reaches in after you through the doorway, and can take you, drawn out through the doorway the way the strand came in before it lifts you. Further in, or round a corner, a building is still safe. Fixed on the way: a giant's strike at anybody indoors was called off the moment it began, so its feeling and tapping at the way in (FIX 552's) never actually played; it does now. |
| 585 | (Q20) Linger as now; improve it if you can | Giving up after forty-five seconds is no longer for good: it goes off about the place, and a minute or two later comes back to where you went in, and stands there looking for you a while -- once. |
| 586 | (Q24) Leviathan strikes: you pick, not rarer | A little more often: its going off into the murk, round behind you and in again each take at most a quarter to a third less long (sixteen, fourteen and twelve seconds), and missed, it goes round again from where its rush carried it rather than all the way off into the murk first. |
| 587 | (Q25) Leviathan and your torch underwater: no effect | It noticed somebody with a torch on faster than somebody without; it no longer cares -- its senses are the water's. (A strider still sees you better lit.) |
| 588 | (Q26) Improve all of their animations so there is more to them, alive -- unnatural and realistic | On top of everything they already do, every creature now: pants after a hard run, fast and deep, its mouth working with it; puts its nose down and works at the ground in short jerks now and then as it wanders; tips its head over to one side watching something; shifts its weight from side to side standing; flicks the end of its tail creeping; and has a shudder run through it now and then. And each moves more or less strangely, from its seed -- a third of them as animals do; the rest more and more uncanny: held stock still a few seconds at a time, not even breathing; their heads tipped further over and snapped there; and the strangest moving their heads in jerks -- held, snapped most of the way, held again -- as nothing with muscles does (not while striking or eating). |
| 589 | (Pictures of the creatures) A leviathan's body stood straight up out of the sea behind its head | Before it had swum its own length, the rest of its body was laid out straight behind along whatever way it had first moved -- rising to its depth, straight up, out of the water. It is laid out level now; and its whole body is kept under the surface (unless it means to come up) and off the sea's floor. And view_head never moved the free camera to the creature: it does now. |
| 590 | The leviathan's body was glitching out, twitching | Its body is laid along the way its head went, and every sharp turn or rise of the head -- just made, a rush begun, a push off something built -- left a corner in that way that ran down the body joint by joint as it swam, each jerked round it in turn (measured: up to 135 m/s2 frame to frame). Its body is now a smooth curve along that way (each joint drawn between its neighbours, then eased from where it was a moment ago, each link kept its length), each joint's sideways turned smoothly too, and it starts with a level way laid out behind it. A new test swims three kinds for thirty seconds and finds nothing of them jerking more than 40 m/s2 between frames. |
| 591 | The leviathan's jaws still aren't attached to the head: moving sideways, its jaw sticks out; and the head with the fins out is floating, not connected | The head was meant to be all on its spine's first bone, its jaw too -- but a long head's back reached the second bone and was put on that one, so as its body bent the back of its skull, its cheeks and the fins and spikes there went one way and the jaw the other. All of the head is on the first bone now, and the body behind it comes off it smoothly over the next bone's length. Its whole body is also kept deep enough that its spines and fins never poke up out of the sea. |
| 592 | The inside of the leviathan's jaw looks wrong, and the front of its face is flat | Its open mouth was the two flat faces where the jaw is cut from the skull, with a red slab between. It has a mouth now: the roof of it arched up into the skull between the rows of upper teeth, a trough down the lower jaw between its rows, a gum left along each lip for the teeth to stand in, and at the back its throat going on down into it -- a wet dark pink, darker further in, the throat going to black. And its snout is rounded off over its last fifth, narrower, lower and shallower towards its end, where the whole head's section was cut off flat. A new command, giant_gape <0 to 1 / off>, holds a leviathan's jaw open to look at it. |
| 593 | (Multiplayer) If any client opens the map it thinks the host is always on it | Who has the navigation map was never written into the message that tells everybody -- every client read player 0, the host, whatever was sent -- and "nobody" could not have been sent anyway (player numbers are three bits; nobody was 255, and a message naming a player past the last was thrown away). It is sent now: whether anybody has it, and who. A test sends nobody and each player and reads them back; another writes every kind of world event and checks each reads back as itself and no further. |
| 594 | (Multiplayer) Both players landed on a different planet: one was oceanic, the other jungle | Joining a game already at a site, the client made the site the moment it was told which site -- before the campaign (which world, where on it) had come -- so it made it as the default: the host's ocean was the client's ice (reproduced: site 2159 oceanic with rain on the host, frozen with snow on the client). The campaign is sent first now, applied before the site is made, and should a site ever have been made without it, it is made again as soon as it comes. Clouds and weather follow from the same world and clock, so they came out different too; with the same site they match (checked: the same fog, cloud deck and spell of rain on both). |
| 595 | (Multiplayer) Other players can't sit if the host is sitting, and can't stand back up at all | The host checked every request to use something against whether it was offered on its own screen -- and a seat is offered only while the host is standing and the seat is free: so with the host sat down nobody could sit, and nobody could ever get up out of their own (occupied) seat. A seat's own rules decide now (anybody may take a free one, and get up out of their own). Tested: the host sits, a client sits beside them, gets up, sits again. |
| 596 | (Multiplayer) When sitting down the head rotation isn't working | Seated, the head turns in the seat, but what was sent to everybody was the way the player faced as they sat. The way the head is turned in the seat is sent now, so the others see it go round. |
| 597 | (Multiplayer) A crewmate fell out of the world for ever as the ship lifted off from the hub | The host took only its own player aboard as the ship lifted off with somebody by the stair, and put only its own player back when they fell out of the world: a client was left on the pad on the host, fell, and fell for ever (1,577 times in a minute: their own game putting them back aboard, the host's putting them back where they were falling). The host does both for everybody now. A command, mp_state, prints what each machine thinks is shared (map, ship, course, site, weather, cinematic, seat, who has the map), to compare; and seat now works from a client too. |
| 598 | When arriving and playing the arriving cutscene, the ship rotates to go over the site: that shouldn't happen until after the cutscene | Its way round the orbit to be over the landing site began the moment it came into orbit -- under the camera. It begins when the arrival has been filmed now. |
| 599 | The flashlight acts weird: I look at a pillar and it snaps to it | The torch, held a hand to the side, is aimed in at whatever is in the middle of the view -- and with the view's middle passing the edge of a pillar, that jumped from the wall beyond to the pillar and back, swinging the beam across. How far ahead it aims in at is eased now (and never nearer than two and a half metres). |
| 600 | The giants wait by doors for too long and don't move on | Twenty seconds at the way in, not forty-five, before it goes off (and comes back once later to look: FIX 585). |
| 601 | Still when opening doors the creature sticks its head through: make it stop before, and open from further away | It saw a shut door in its way only a metre and a half ahead of its feet -- by then its head, two metres and more out in front, was at the door, and through it. It sees one as far ahead as its head reaches and more now, stops there, and works the door from out at arm's length. The lab's door test now checks its head never goes past the shut door. |
| 602 | If a building is destroyed, we don't need an airlock in the destruction | A half-fallen building has no airlocks now: broken open, there is no air in it to keep. |
| 603 | Make it sound like you are in space if you go into a building with holes, where it makes sense | Inside a half-fallen building on a world without air, everything is heard as it is outside -- through the suit, as in space -- not as under a roof. |
| 604 | Feel like I shouldn't be able to see the blue through the snow for the horizon | Below the horizon -- what shows past the far edge of the land -- the sky was drawn the ground's own dark blue-grey, whatever the fog: in a snowfall that stood as a blue band along the horizon between the white land and the white sky. It is lost in the fog with everything else now, as the fog closes in and in a thick haze. |
| 605 | The leviathan still has floating stuff on the face, you can see where the mouth connects at the back, and the mouth has another lip or piece inside it | A sea dragon's whiskers hang from its snout down past the mouth line -- and were cut in two by it, the part under it going with the jaw: open, they came apart in pieces floating by its face. They are all the head's now. The gums left along the lips inside the mouth (FIX 592) stood up as a second lip: half as wide now, and the dark band painted along the mouth line, which made it look like another lip inside the first, is gone. |
| 606 | Still the tentacles are twisting and flinging around; they shouldn't glitch like this. And they look inverted | Inverted: every face of their tubes was wound to look inwards, so with the near side of a tube not drawn, what showed was the inside of its far side -- inside out. They face out now. Flinging: measured, links jumped up to 3.4 m in one frame (12,000 m/s2): the shape the working strand is drawn to (where it reaches, where it looks, how far into a strike) changed at once, and the steps that keep a strand together pulled its links one way and another from frame to frame. The shape is eased to now, and each link eased to where it is put and never moved faster than a strand can (over and above its root's going with the body). A new test walks three striders hunting someone for twenty-five seconds: nothing of their strands jerks more than 400 m/s2 between frames (a strike's whip out from rest excepted). |
| 607 | Make sure the giants have animations as well, like the jaw and stuff | A strider's head had a mouth cut along its muzzle that never opened. Its lower jaw is its own now, hinged at the back of the mouth (its teeth stay on the skull, over the lip): a little open as it breathes, working as it hunts, wide as it strikes, chewing what it eats, slack when dead, shut when it holds stock still. And it breathes: its body swelling and easing, slow, deeper as it hunts. |
| 608 | Had a sign generate like this (a dark branching thing bent round a box of nothing): that's not right | The organism's signs climb the buildings they reach -- and took each building as its whole footprint, as high as all its floors: one bigger under the ground than over it, or buried but for its entrance, had a sign climbing walls that are not there, bent round a box of nothing. They climb only what stands over the ground now, cell by cell, as high as it stands there. (The sign test had been placed by one of those walls that is not there; it is placed by a standing one now.) |
| 609 | We still have lighter terrain on the ground when the facility is bigger underground | Over the buried part of a building the land runs a few centimetres over its roof -- but stopped half a metre short of the part standing over it, and the roof showed there along the foot of its walls as a pale strip. The land runs right up to the wall now (the wall's own thickness hides where it stops). |
| 610 | The ladder has some of the terrain render over it | In a building with floors under the ground the land is cut away under it only a cell in from its walls -- so a ladder's hatch (or a flight's well, a lift's shaft) near a wall had the land, just under the ground floor, lying across it. Every way up or down through the ground floor has the land cut away over it now. |
| 611 | Is there a way to improve stuff from far away? My game looks noisy far away | Nothing smoothed the picture's edges at all (multisampling off, and no other kind): every far pole, tree, wall's edge and blade was a stair of pixels crawling as the view moved. The finished picture is smoothed now (FXAA): where its brightness steps from one pixel to the next, the step is blended along the way the edge runs. On by default; r.fxaa 0 turns it off. And far cloud towards the horizon is marched in twice as many, shorter steps: at two dozen, half a kilometre each, it came out as blobs striped with where the steps fell. |
| 612 | Is the horizon realistic, like the white? | A paler band low in the sky is: the air seen through edge on, far more of it. Ours was two and a half times the fog's brightness, a hard white glare along every horizon; it is a little paler than the fog now. |
| 613 | You can see the creature's silhouette way too well with the flashlight (underwater) | The torch's beam glowed in the water all the way out to thirty metres -- so anything out in it cut the glow short, and stood black against the lit water behind it. In water only the first seven metres of the beam glow now: past them everything is lost in the murk alike. |
| 614 | (Multiplayer) Make sure all the commands work for multiplayer too, like test site and spawning stuff | test_site was refused in a game played together (it changed only one machine's site). The host makes a test site for everybody now: what it is made as (biome, ground, water) goes to the others before the site does, so everybody makes the same one (tested: host and client both on site 7, jungle, rain). A client asking is told it is the host's to make. Spawning creatures from a client already worked (the host makes them). |
| 615 | When loading, the black loading screen doesn't fit the full screen | The loading screen (and the console) were sized in the window's pixels, while the interface works in its own units scaled with the window (FIX 571): on a window shorter than the interface is laid out for, the black fell short of the right and the bottom (the same as the gap on the right in the intro's fade). Both are sized in the interface's own units now. |
| 616 | There is a line in the clouds | FIX 611 marched the far cloud in twice as many steps past six kilometres -- and where the one count met the other, a line ran across the sky (the cloud's finest detail, which goes by the step's length, changing there too). The steps now lengthen smoothly with the distance, never longer than three hundred metres, as many as that takes. |
| 617 | The creature just kept changing its mind -- ran away, chased me, ran away -- kept switching to approaching from the side, and never attacked me even after I shot it | Reproduced in a test (a big predator shot at from thirty metres in the open): one of six changed its mind 19 times in 20 seconds. Half making out the person it was already chasing -- the one who had just shot it -- it took them for "something caught its eye" and went to look; and every round sent it to creep up on them from cover there was none of, then back off, then chase again. A glimpse about where somebody it is after just was (or who just shot it) is now simply them, and nothing creeps up on somebody who is shooting at it. The hit-and-run kind struck and turned to run after every blow, then straight back in at the next round (59 changes in 30 seconds): now it really goes, for three and a half seconds at least, and only once every 14 to 22 seconds. Coming in on a slant it keeps to a side for two to three and a half seconds, not one. All six now go straight for the shooter (at most three changes, there in under 14 seconds; a test). (Your screenshot: "Retreat, getting away" with no fear was the hit-and-run; tiredness only sends it to sleep when nothing is about.) |
| 618 | Still the airlocks are broken: they open up to a wall | An airlock built inside the building (into the room behind the door out) was stretched to the room's far wall when the room was only a little deeper than it -- so as not to leave a slot behind it (FIX 584) -- and its door into the building was in that far wall, opening onto the room's own wall 35 cm away. Found in 7 of 40 buildings by a new test. Now it is shortened instead, always leaving 1.8 m to walk out of it into the room; the test walks a metre and a half through both doors of every airlock in 40 buildings and finds nothing in the way. |
| 619 | The giant grabbed me through the airlock | Whether a strider could reach in after somebody through the way they went in was asked of the level alone -- and doors are not the level (they move), so a shut door, an airlock's among them, was no bar to it. A shut door across the line is now as solid as a wall to it (anybody, any creature and its own strands still let the line through). A test: six striders waiting outside somebody just in through a shut door never take them. |
| 620 | The giants stamping by where you went in doesn't make sense, and the leg clips through the building | Gone. A strider with no strands, waiting where somebody went in, now goes slowly round outside it -- a step or two at a time, stopping, bent down to look in, now from one side and now another -- and moves on after its twenty seconds as before. (With strands it still feels at the way in, and reaches in after somebody standing just inside with nothing shut between.) |
| 621 | The giants still sometimes have a leg lag behind | Two ways: a strider that stands dead still while it is looked at froze its feet where they were, so a foot it had been about to bring in stayed out behind it as long as it was watched -- now it brings any trailing foot in under it first, then holds still; and walking, a foot waiting its turn could trail a stride and a half before it had to be taken (half the giant's height on a tall one) -- now a little over one. |
| 622 | When being grabbed the camera still goes back and forth really fast | Held, which way you face was set twice every step: once by where you look (turned toward what has you, or by the mouse), and again by whatever holds you -- the way the creature or giant faced. The picture showed now the one and now the other, many times a second, wherever they differed (a giant turning as it carries you, most of all). Once held, the hold now only moves you; which way you face is left to where you look. (Checked in the lab: held and dragged off for fourteen seconds, the view turns smoothly one way to what has you, and the eye moves a few centimetres a frame.) |
| 623 | When the leviathan has seen something it stops in place | It stopped dead in the water, as a strider stops where it stands, for as long as it was making out what it had seen. Now it glides on, slowing, turning its head and then itself towards it. |
| 624 | If the leviathan is close, the going around out of sight doesn't really make sense | However close it already was, it first swam off to well past what can be seen, round behind where they were looking, and back in. Now, within about twenty metres -- or anywhere they are not looking its way (to one side, behind) -- it comes straight in at them. Only somebody watching it from further off sees it turn away into the murk to come round. (A test: close, behind somebody looking away, it never goes off, and goes for them.) |
| 625 | Leviathans still have a lip inside the middle of their mouth; the red at the bottom, and the jaw doesn't smoothly go into the body | Its lower jaw was built apart from its head, a little up into the head all along its length (so shut, the join was hidden inside) and cut off it at the back by a flat wall at its hinge, painted dark red. Open, the strip that had been inside the head came down with the jaw as a lip over its lower teeth, and the wall showed as a red band under its throat. Now head and jaw are one skin, parted only along the mouth: all of what is under the mouth line in front goes with the jaw, and behind the corners of the mouth less and less towards the back and the mouth line -- the skin of its throat stretching as it opens, as a snake's does. The dark red is only along the lips' edges now. Lower teeth go with the jaw as much as the gum they stand in. |
| 626 | The front of the mouth: the lip is connected | The mouth line stopped a little short of the end of the snout, so with the head and jaw one skin the snout's end stretched from lip to lip across the front of the open mouth. The mouth is now parted right through the front of the snout. |
| 627 | All the giants have the same head pretty much | They did: one skull, its muzzle a little longer or shorter, its dome and brows a little higher or heavier, horns or a ridge or not. Now five kinds of head, from the giant's seed: the long skull as it was; a hammerhead, its eyes out at the ends of lobes either side; a blunt one, short in the face, wide, heavy in the brow and the jaw; a crested one, long and narrow in the muzzle, a blade of crest swept back over its neck; and a shovel, flat and wide, its eyes up on top. (Chosen apart from its other numbers, so nothing else about any giant changed.) |
| 628 | When entering the planet, the water doesn't show up until you get close | The site's water was drawn only with the eye within two and a half kilometres of the site, across the ground -- and the shuttle's run in begins tens of kilometres out, so coming down the sea's floor showed where the sea should be until the last minute. The water is now drawn as far off as the land is, whenever the eye is at the site. |
| 629 | You can see the edge of the world, so it doesn't look like an infinite planet | High over a site, its land fades away at its rim -- but what showed past it was the sky's own colour under the horizon, which matched nothing, so the land stood out as a square with a pale plain round it. Past the rim now is the very colour the land fades into. And out to sea (round an island, over the sea's floor, half of a coast) that colour is the sea's, as the water is drawn from high up -- it was the colour of the site's sand, so the sea came round to a beige plain far off. (Checked coming down to three worlds: no square edge on any.) |
| 630 | (Multiplayer) Make sure all the commands work for multiplayer too | site_weather changed the weather on the one machine it was typed on. Played together it is now the host's to set and everybody's to see: the host sends what falls to everybody (and to anybody who joins later); typed on a client, it asks the host, who does it for all. (Tested with two machines: the host's snow, then the client's rain, on both.) |
| 631 | The waves look weird / the view flips underwater when a wave passes | Under water or not was one answer for the whole picture, taken from a little over the surface at the eye (12 cm over, for how the drawn waves sit against the worked-out ones) -- so with the eye bobbing just over the water the picture flipped to under it and back as the waves went by. Now the picture is the air's until the whole lens is under (about 8 cm down; what is heard and the breath still go by the head, as before); and while the surface crosses the eye, the water's line is drawn across the picture where the surface cuts the lens, sloping as the wave does -- below it the murk, wavering, lost in the water's colour with distance, a bright edge along the line. A wave going by sweeps up across the view and down again. (Checked coming up from six metres down: under, the sky through the surface; the line crossing; then the air.) |
| 632 | You can get rid of the README.txt that gets packaged with the game | The package no longer writes one. |
| 633 | Other players still can't stand when you land | A client getting up asks the host, and the host checked the seat was within reach of where it last had them -- which, carried down in the shuttle, could still be where they sat down up in the ship's hangar; out of reach, it was ignored, and only the host could ever stand. Getting up out of one's own seat is never out of reach now. (Two machines, landed: the client's seat stayed taken before; now it is freed.) |
| 634 | None of the cutscenes are working for the clients | Whether a cutscene is filmed (the camera taken out to watch the ship take off, arrive, set down) was never written into the message that tells the others it has started -- so on every client it played unfilmed: the ship moved, the picture stayed on their own eyes. It is sent now (a test). |
| 635 | The block next to the facility: no collision, no door | A part of a lower wing with nothing in it over the ground on any storey -- no room, no corridor, no stair -- is built as a closed block against the building, clad and roofed. It was not solid all through: walked into from some sides, there was nothing there. It is now (rays into such blocks from every side hit them). (Taking those parts off the building instead changed where everything else on the site goes, and pipes ran into walls.) |
| 636 | The giants: a lot of stuff isn't syncing, like its position (it is lower) and its tentacle position | Only where it was, which way it faced, what it was doing, what it looked at and its blow were sent; everything else its body shows was the host's thinking, worked out again (differently) on every other machine: how far it bends down to look in (so it stood at another height), standing dead still while watched, reaching in at a doorway and where, whom it holds and how -- eating, lowered to throw, how far through its throw, drawing them out through the doorway. All of it is sent now (a test), and the strand doing the work -- reaching, striking, holding, reaching in -- is shaped by it the same everywhere. (Two machines with a strider: its place and its head's height agree to a metre. The strands only hanging and swaying still sway each their own way.) mp_state now prints the giant too. |
| 637 | When sitting, the flashlights don't seem to sync | The others' torches were drawn from where the host last had them standing -- sitting in the shuttle, that could be back where they sat down, up in the ship: nowhere near the cabin. Sitting, a torch is now drawn from their seat's eye, the shuttle as it is carried on this machine. |
| 638 | Make sure everyone has to be in the ship | Only the helm's setting out checked that everybody was aboard, and only with the ship on the ground. A course set straight from the map in orbit -- with somebody still down at the site -- or by the course command, set out with them left behind. Every way of setting out now needs everybody up to be aboard, and says who is not. (Two machines: refused with the client out on the dock; set out once both were aboard, the take-off filmed on both.) |
| 639 | Get rid of the flashing lights around the creatures | Some creatures had rows of lights along their flanks, pulsing. Gone, and everything that made them (a giant still has its own). Their eyes still shine back in a torch. |
| 640 | Its body was breathing really fast and its head movements were teleporting -- is this intentional? | Not meant to look like that. Panting after a run went to well over a breath a second, the jaw working faster still, and sniffing shook the head at six a second: about half that now. And the "strange" ones held their head still and then snapped it most of the way to where it was going, which read as the head jumping from place to place: that is gone, and a twitch of the head is softer. |
| 641 | When walking, sometimes its foot steps, goes back up and comes down, like it skipped | Where a foot was going to come down was chosen as it lifted; a body going on faster than the step left the foot behind as it landed, and up it came again at once. A test of six creatures walking and running: 2 skipped steps in 322 before, none now. |
| 642 | The leviathan's head was spinning 360 in place, flipping 180 in place, twisting and contorting; when it went to attack me it swam back and forth in place | Something behind it, it turned on the spot: it slowed to a third of its speed while it turned, so it turned nearly where it was, its head swinging round and its body knotting behind it; and missing a rush it turned straight back at once, to and fro. | 643 | It was trying to attack me on the platform like it could see me, swimming in circles | Somebody gone up out of the water, it went round and round under where they went, thirty metres out, for twenty seconds. | 644 | It still tries to go around me out of sight when it's really close | It came straight in only within about twenty metres. Now anywhere within nine-tenths of how far it would have swum off to (forty-odd metres), or wherever it is when they are not looking its way, it comes straight in. |
| 645 | The lights were clipping through the surface because the facility was close to the surface | The lamps of a building under the sea lit the sea's surface over them as if they were over it -- bright patches mirrored on the water. A lamp on the other side of the surface from the eye now shows only as a faint glow through the water between, dimmed the more water there is: nothing mirrored, nothing lighting the top. |
| 646 | Looking up from underwater still isn't right: I see a full sky of clouds, clearer than the water | Through the surface from under it the sky's clouds were sharp and bright. They are bent about by the waves now, soft-edged and fainter, and the murk of the water between is over the whole window -- more of it the deeper. |
| 647 | Make it look more wet at the surface and underwater | Water on the visor: coming up out of the water it streams with drops that bend what is behind them and slide down at their own pace, trails over them, running off over five seconds; out in rain, a few drops always. (With the waterline across the view at the surface, FIX 631.) |
| 648 | Far away it looks very pixelated | Two causes. High over a site, its land and water at their rim were left out pixel by pixel more and more towards the edge: from far off that was a band of speckle along the horizon.
